# C-family aggregate — ELAI deep feature discovery

Family: **C** (verification-gap, testing-trust, code-completeness, paper-grounded extensions C-1..C-59).
Inputs: DEEP-C-001..DEEP-C-006 + `03-C-SUMMARY_INDEX.discovery.md`.
Rule: every distinct atomic record is preserved with namespaced ID and source anchor.
NEWER-PLAN PRECEDENCE: explicit SUPERSEDED/replacement/status-override markers win; else higher C-N plan number is newer target state. Losing descriptions remain as superseded notes.

## Notation

- Namespaced ID form: `C-00N/<local-id>` or `SUMMARY/S-Ennnn`.
- Cross-file interactions, summary deltas, and ambiguity/conflict registers keep local IDs under their ledger namespace (e.g. `C-002/IX-001`, `C-004/D-C8-001`, `C-001/A-014`).
- `[E]` explicit, `[I]` inferred, `[A]` ambiguous, `[C]` conflicting, `[Δ]/`[D+]`/`[D~]` summary delta, `[status]`, `[superseded]`.
- `SURFACE` marks user-facing windows, panels, commands, CLI verbs, flags, status displays, notifications (feeds dashboard DESIGN.md).
- Plan sources remain PLANNED unless a ledger explicitly records shipped status (e.g. C-5).

## User-facing surfaces

Every atom that names a window, panel, drawer, command, CLI verb, flag, status display, or notification. Full text retained; reference by namespaced ID.

Surface-tagged atom count: **366**.

- **C-003/E-C11-001** [feature] [security] (unit=C-11, ledger=C-003 | anchor: L1-L5, L30-L31, L47-L67): [security] In Linux `clone_cb`, apply capability bounding-set drops, five resource limits, `/proc` masking, and existing seccomp, with seccomp required last.

- **C-003/E-C11-003** [feature] [implementation constraint] (unit=C-11, ledger=C-003 | anchor: L49-L54): [implementation constraint] Rlimits use stack `libc::rlimit` plus named constants; no heap allocation or magic numbers.
- **C-003/E-C11-004** [feature] [isolation/failure] (unit=C-11, ledger=C-003 | anchor: L56-L60): [isolation/failure] Mount `proc` on `/proc` with `MS_NOSUID|MS_NOEXEC|MS_NODEV` and `hidepid=2` in the private `CLONE_NEWNS` child; every mount failure, including `EPERM`, `_exit(123)`.
- **C-003/E-C11-005** [feature] [test fallback] (unit=C-11, ledger=C-003 | anchor: L58-L60): [test fallback] Unprivileged CI is expected to hit `EPERM`; existing `CLONE_NEWNS::need_cap` guards skip those tests.

- **C-002/C27-E08** [feature] [config/default] (unit=C-27, ledger=C-002 | anchor: C-27:L77-L82): `from_env` reads `ELAI_SEED` defaulting to 0 and `ELAI_HARDWARE_CLASS` defaulting to Unknown. `matches_fingerprint` returns true when no pin is configured. [C-27:L77-L82]
- **C-002/C27-E09** [feature] [determinism/security] (unit=C-27, ledger=C-002 | anchor: C-27:L78-L81; C-27:L189-L191): The eight-byte prefix is the first eight bytes of `blake3::hash(seed.to_le_bytes())`; `prefix_cache_key` pads it to 32 bytes and uses `blake3::keyed_hash`. - **C-002/C27-E13** [feature] [failure] (unit=C-27, ledger=C-002 | anchor: C-27:L96-L97): `WiringContractError` variants are parse, I/O-from-`io::Error`, and regex errors; all core contract types derive Debug/Clone/Serialize/Deserialize. [C-27:L96-L97]
- **C-002/C27-E14** [feature] [config/fallback] (unit=C-27, ledger=C-002 | anchor: C-27:L99-L104): `EntrypointSetLoader {config_path}` reads TOML `[wiring.entrypoints] symbols`; a missing section returns an empty set with `tracing::warn!`, never panic. - **C-002/C27-E15** [feature] [security/audit] (unit=C-27, ledger=C-002 | anchor: C-27:L106-L113): `StubBanAuditor` compiles regexes and emits `WiringViolation(StubPattern)` with file/line. #### C-35 — feature (44)

- **C-005/C35-E01** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L1-L12): The objective is per-FSM-state tool visibility plus on-demand full schemas to cut repeated MCP catalog prompt cost; Phase-4K is required, C-35 precedes overlapping C-42, and C-36/37/38/39 are parallel-safe. [L1-L12]
- **C-005/C35-E02** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L14-L22): The cited paper’s roughly 95% tool-token reduction is treated as measured, but downstream quality claims are explicitly projections requiring ELAI’s own SSB accuracy comparison. [L14-L22]
- **C-005/C35-E03** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L33-L37): Dynamic gating intersects registered tools with a state allowlist; tools outside the active visible set are represented by one aggregate “N tools available on request” marker rather than full schemas. [L33-L37]
- **C-005/C35-E04** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L38-L41): Lazy loading puts only tool name + one-line summary in the prompt and resolves full parameter/return JSON only after a call references that tool, targeting a catalog reduction from roughly 6–10K to 300–500 tokens. [L38-L41]
- **C-005/C35-E05** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L55-L60): Source grounding of arXiv:2604.21816 into `tool-attention-interface.md` is required before code changes. [L55-L60]
- **C-005/C35-E06** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L62-L66): `[tool_gating]` declares `enabled` (true after acceptance), `gate_manifest_path="plugins/tool-gate.toml"`, `lazy_schema_default=true`, and `max_eager_schemas=4`, validated at startup. [L62-L66]
- **C-005/C35-E07** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L68-L82): Each `[gate.STATE]` manifest section separates `eager` (full schema), `gated` (name/summary), and `denied` (omitted and structured-error on attempted call). [L68-L82]
- **C-005/C35-E08** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L84-L87): `ToolGate::load_gate` and `filter_for_state(fsm_state,full_catalog)` produce `GatedCatalog { eager_schemas: Vec<FullSchema>, gated_summaries: Vec<SummaryRow>, denied_names: HashSet<String> }`. [L84-L87]
- **C-005/C35-E09** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L89-L92): `server.rs` replaces full-catalog rendering with state-gated rendering and resolves a referenced gated tool through `resolve_schema_on_demand(tool_name)`. [L89-L92]
- **C-005/C35-E10** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L92-L94): A lazily resolved schema is injected only for the current call response cycle unless active-use LRU promotion keeps it eager; the LRU capacity is `max_eager_schemas`. [L92-L94]
- **C-005/C35-E11** [feature] [E] (unit=C-35, ledger=C-005 | anchor: L96-L98): `LazySchemaCache` uses LRU eviction and a per-tool first-load lock to prevent a concurrent thundering herd, and records hit/miss/resolve-latency metrics. [L96-L98]

- **C-002/I01** [inferred] (unit=CROSS, ledger=C-002): Schema sequencing. C-1, C-5, C-27, C-42, C-43, C-50, and C-57 all mutate the SSB event family. Their changes therefore need a single migration/order strategy even where individual sources claim parallel safety; fixed version literals cannot be applied independently. This follows from the shared schema surfaces, but no unified migration plan is stated.
- **C-002/I02** [inferred] (unit=CROSS, ledger=C-002): Spend enforcement race. C-5's read-before-route cap check is not described as a reservation/transaction. Concurrent cloud calls could all read a below-cap value and cross the ceiling together unless the implementation adds serialization or an atomic spend reservation.
- **C-002/I03** [inferred] (unit=CROSS, ledger=C-002): Daily-warning idempotency. “No prior row this calendar day” implies a stable day boundary and concurrency-safe deduplication key/transaction; neither is specified.
- **C-002/I04** [inferred] (unit=CROSS, ledger=C-002): Cache-counter concurrency. C-1's hit/miss mutation plus flush-and-reset needs synchronization if `VectorCache` is shared, but counter atomicity/locking is not stated.
- **C-002/I05** [inferred] (unit=CROSS, ledger=C-002): Project-index concurrency. C-4's temp-file/rename makes each replacement crash-resistant, but without locking two concurrent `create_project` calls can still lose an append.
- **C-002/I06** [inferred] (unit=CROSS, ledger=C-002 | SURFACE): Audit correlation. C-12's started-before-verdict contract implies rows can be correlated by mode/tier/task/agent and sequence, although no explicit correlation ID is introduced.
